9	
10	The core_node task pipeline spans four subsystems: laravel_main's generic **GlobalTask** system (`global_tasks` table, `TaskManagerService`, no-auth worker HTTP API `/api/worker/{register,tasks/pull,tasks/result,heartbeat}`, append-only `GlobalTaskEvent` audit), **pycore** (callmodule worker services + threadpool), **chrome-mcp** (task-center `SimpleWorkerBase` + `WorkerApiClient`), and the **wordflow UI** (`poly_apps/pycore_laravel_wordflow_ui` React Task Center).
11	
12	Non-obvious truths (cost real investigation; the surface "everything is GlobalTask" is wrong):
13	- There were historically **two task substrates**. (A) GlobalTask — **word images** flow here via `AppQyV1WordMediaService`. (B) AppQyV1 dict-column queues (`tts_status`/`tts_priority`/`image_status` columns on the `{prefix}_tts_cache_{lang}` rows) drained by Octane timers — **translation** and **word audio** flow here; **sentence audio is generated synchronously inline** (no row, no task).
14	- **Audio is generated by pycore via the ASSIST protocol** (`tts/worker/claim` + `tts/worker/report` on dict rows), NOT the GlobalTask worker pull API. The Laravel-local TTS timer (`AppQyV1TTSGenerationTask`) is OFF by default (`APPQYV1_TTS_AUTO_GENERATION=false`).
15	- Realtime is **SSE, not Reverb** — `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=log`; the pattern to clone is `AppQyV1TranslationStreamController` (`response()->eventStream()` + cursor + DB-outbox tail).
16	- DB is **PostgreSQL** (`core_node_main`, connection `main`); migrations use `SafeMigrationHelper` (idempotent add-only, no destructive `down()`). Feature flags are read as `(bool) env('APPQYV1_*', default)`.
17

1	# Unified Task System Upgrade — Implementation Plan
2	
3	> Generated from the two-phase mapping + design workflows (2026-06-21). Spans **laravel_main**, **pycore**, **chrome-mcp**, and the **wordflow UI** (wordnew app).
4	> Goal: a stronger cross-subsystem task pipeline for translate / audio / image, a live per-task **detail UI**, and a **fast `task-top`** path for interactive word/sentence requests handled by **both** the pycore and chrome-mcp clients (capability-typed, first-idle-wins).
5	
6	## Locked decisions
7	- **Dual-client** = one shared `remote_fast` execution_type both clients register for; **first-idle-wins**, runs exactly once; tasks are **capability-typed** (pycore = `audio`,`translate`; chrome-mcp = `image`,`translate`,`sentence_audio`). No fan-out / no duplicate work.
8	- **Fast trigger** = explicit FE flag (`interactive=true`) on user-initiated single word/sentence requests. No length heuristic.
9	- **Task detail** = live drilldown **modal over SSE** (clone the existing `AppQyV1TranslationStreamController` eventStream + cursor pattern; Reverb is NOT used — `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=log`).
10	- **Scope** = full build + **substrate unification** (merge the AppQyV1 dictionary-column queues + `assist_requests` into `GlobalTask`), via a strictly reversible migration.
11	
12	## Architecture today

## What I found before launching

This isn't a greenfield build — the **2026-06-21 unified-task-system upgrade already shipped** (Phases 1–7), and the audio interactive path was wired earlier today. Your request is the *continuation*: make the routing logic strong and actually work, build out the task-detail UIs, and verify all logic + overflow. The real architecture (verified against code, not just docs):

- **The routing gate exists** — `interactive=true` on a user lookup promotes the request onto a shared `remote_fast` lane with a capability tag:
  - **translate** → `remote_fast` GlobalTask, `capability=translate` (either client can claim)
  - **image** → already flows through GlobalTask (`capability=image`, chrome)
  - **audio** → deliberately **not** `remote_fast` — it uses the assist protocol (`tts_priority=100` front-bump) because a `remote_fast` audio task has no writeback processor. This is the subtle part of your "audio goes to task-top" requirement.
- **Both clients race** the `remote_fast` lane via atomic `lockForUpdate` (first-idle-wins, no duplicate work), filtered by capability in PHP (Postgres-safe).
- **Task-detail UIs + live SSE** exist (`/api/task/{id}/{detail,stream}`, `TaskDetailModal.vue`, `QueuePanel.tsx`), cloned from the proven `AppQyV1TranslationStreamController` pattern.

## What the audit is verifying right now

The 7 dimensions target exactly your concerns — **"logic must be strong / must work" + "verify all logic and overflow"**:

1. **Routing gate** — does *only* translate/image/audio reach task-top, with the correct capability, and everything else stays off?
2. **Claim/concurrency/overflow** — the `min(32, need+8)` over-fetch silent cap, the `countFastPending` nested-`orWhere` SQL-precedence risk, priority integer overflow, double-claim races.
3. **SSE/detail** — lifetime clamp, cursor resume, terminal close, busy-loop risk.
4. **pycore** — caps `[audio,translate]` exactly, fast-drain re-poll, unbounded heap/ring-buffer overflow.
5. **chrome-mcp** — the big open issue: each worker subclass must advertise *only* caps it can execute, or a fast task it can't run silently times out.
6. **Task-detail UIs** — do the modals actually stream and render incrementally, with EventSource cleanup?
7. **Cross-system contracts** — capability vocab, endpoint paths, field names (`pending_fast`), SSE event names must match *exactly* across all four subsystems or fast requests silently stall.

Every finding gets independently refuted before I trust it, so I act only on real defects. When it lands I'll triage the confirmed findings, then run the fix + re-verify workflows. I'll wait for the completion notification rather than poll.

[2026-06-22 20:57:02] ASSISTANT:
The full audit is in. **24 confirmed findings, 5 refuted** (the refuted ones — e.g. the "generic `/api/task/create` escape hatch" and "pycore subscribe unwired" — were correctly knocked down: no real caller, or an incomplete grep that missed `.tsx`). Here's the triage that matters:

## Why it "doesn't fully work" — the root causes (all HIGH, all silent)

1. **`CONTRACT-FE-TRANSLATE-1`** — wordnew's interactive translate fast-track omits the required `language` field → Laravel 422 → the `remote_fast` translate task is **never created**. Fire-and-forget, so no error shown.
2. **`CONTRACT-FE-AUDIO-2`** — wordnew's interactive audio fast-track sends `{texts}` to a route that requires `{tasks:[{content,language}]}` → 422 → the word-audio front-bump **never happens**.
3. **`PYCORE-FAST-1`** — pycore's pull never sends `wait=0`, so it hits Laravel's 20s long-poll while the client times out at 8s → idle **re-register churn every 12s** and the 0.5s fast-drain cadence is silently degraded to a 20s long-poll.
4. **`SSE-CLOSE-CONTRACT-1`** — `stream.close` can't distinguish "task done" from "lifetime cap hit", so the wordnew modal **reconnects forever** on a finished task while chrome **stops updating** a long task.
5. **`LARAVEL-CONC-1`** — `releaseTimedOutTasks()` has no row lock/transaction → a task completing near its timeout gets clobbered back to `pending` → **double execution** (duplicate image/translation writeback).

## The routing-spec gap you specifically called out

6. **`LARAVEL-GATE-2` / `CHROME-CAP-2`** — **image never reaches the task-top**. Only translate actually hits the fast lane today; image stays on the normal `remote_client` lane. (Physical constraint I'll be honest about: pycore has no image generator, so image fast-races to **chrome only** — "both clients at the same time" is literally true only for translate. I'll wire image to the fast tier so it's claimed sub-second, which is the achievable version of your intent.)

Plus the gate is **confirmed correct** where it's wired: translate→`remote_fast`+`capability=translate` (either client, writeback preserved), audio→assist `tts_priority=100` bump (correct by design), non-interactive stays off the fast lane, atomic first-idle-wins with no fan-out.

Rest are MEDIUM/LOW correctness + UI polish (chrome silent-drop on task_type mismatch, offline-worker multi-task orphaning, shape-gate keyed on rewritten `execution_type`, priority-aging foot-gun, Carbon-3 float seconds, badge colors, etc.).

I'll now run the **fix workflow** — 4 agents partitioned by subsystem (disjoint file sets, no conflicts), each given the verified fix and the shared `stream.close {cursor, done}` contract, each self-verifying its subsystem.

6 pass, 1 fail. The single defect: **`WorkerController::pullTasks` reads `$wait` as the string `"0"`** (Laravel 12 doesn't cast query params), so `if ($wait === 0)` strict-compares `"0" === 0` → false → the request falls through to the 20s long-poll. My pycore `wait=0` fix is correct on the client, but the server never honors it. This is the kind of cross-seam defect that only an end-to-end read catches — exactly why the seam verifier exists. Let me read the region and patch it.

- **translate** → `remote_fast` + `capability=translate` → **both** pycore & chrome race (first-idle-wins, atomic, no double-run). ✅

1	# Code Sync — Lightweight Standalone Library (Architecture Design)
2	
3	> Status: **IMPLEMENTED** — the library lives at `pycore/pyutils/codesync/`
4	> (bootstrap `pycore/pyutils/codesync_boot.py`); the old `device_sync/code_sync_*`
5	> + `peer_*` modules were removed and all references repointed. Usage doc:
6	> `pycore/pyutils/codesync/README.md`. This file remains the architecture record.
7	>
8	> Target: extract the existing Code Sync
9	> mesh into a self-contained, **stdlib-only** package that can run on its own
10	> (`pyservice.sh codesync ...`) without importing `third_party`, without booting
11	> the full `pycore` runtime, and without the `pyservice.sh` prerequisite install —
12	> while the full `pycore` runtime keeps using the **same** library. Linux + Windows.
13	
14	---
15	
16	## 1. Goals & hard constraints
17	
18	1. **Same logic, one source of truth.** The peer config, role mesh, file
19	   distribution (dev → client) and last-writer-wins replication behave exactly as
20	   documented in `CODE_SYNC_MESH.md`. No behavioural change.
21	2. **Standalone = stdlib only.** When launched as `pyservice.sh codesync …`, the
22	   process imports **only the Python 3 standard library**. No `requests`, no
23	   `fastapi`/`uvicorn`, no `pycore` package import, no `third_party`.
24	3. **No prerequisites, no `pycore_module_caller`.** The `codesync` launch branch in
25	   `pyservice.sh` / `pyservice.ps1` must short-circuit **before** the prereq
26	   (`prepare.sh`) step and must never run `pycore_module_caller.py` ("pymain").
27	4. **Shared by full pycore.** When the full native pycore runtime is running,
28	   `pyservice.sh codesync` (and the in-process FastAPI router) call the **same**
29	   library — pycore just *injects* its richer services (logging, event bus,
30	   machine-id) into the library at startup.
31	5. **Cross-OS.** Identical behaviour on Linux / macOS / Git-Bash / WSL (`.sh`) and
32	   Windows PowerShell (`.ps1`). Stdlib only ⇒ no platform-specific deps.

        f"split, number, annotate or omit any line. Output nothing but the JSON array."

1	# codesync — lightweight, stdlib-only Code Sync
2	
3	A self-contained Code Sync mesh (role-based dev → client file distribution +
4	last-writer-wins peer-config replication) that runs in **two modes from one
5	codebase**:
6	
7	- **Standalone** — `pyservice.sh codesync ...` (Linux/macOS/Git-Bash/WSL) or
8	  `.\pyservice.ps1 codesync ...` (Windows). Imports **only the Python standard
9	  library**: no `requests`, no FastAPI, no `third_party`, no pycore. No
10	  prerequisite install runs.
11	- **Inside full pycore** — imported as `pycore.pyutils.codesync`; pycore injects
12	  its logging / event-bus / shutdown via `configure()` and serves `/code-sync/*`
13	  from its FastAPI app. Same implementation, same committed peer file.
14	
15	Behaviour is unchanged from the previous `device_sync/code_sync_*` modules — see
16	`docs/CODE_SYNC_MESH.md` for the model and `docs/CODESYNC_LITE_DESIGN.md`
17	for the extraction architecture.

114	def translate_lines(
115	    lines: List[str],
116	    src_lang: str,
117	    dest_lang: str,
118	    *,
119	    provider: Optional[str] = None,
120	    chunk_size: int = DEFAULT_CHUNK_SIZE,
121	    domain: str = "text",
122	    source: str = "ai_batch_translate",
123	    progress: Optional[Callable[[int, int], None]] = None,
124	) -> Optional[List[str]]:
125	    """Translate an arbitrary-length ``lines`` src->dest in chunks.
126	
127	    Chunks ``lines`` (default 80/call), translates each via ``translate_chunk``,
128	    and returns the concatenated index-aligned list the SAME length as ``lines``.
129	    Returns None if ANY chunk fails (so the caller can leave that language
130	    unfilled). ``progress(done, total)`` is called after each chunk. NEVER raises.
131	    """
132	    out: List[str] = []
133	    total = len(lines)
134	    if total == 0:
135	        return []
136	    size = max(1, int(chunk_size or DEFAULT_CHUNK_SIZE))
137	    for start in range(0, total, size):
138	        part = lines[start:start + size]
139	        arr = translate_chunk(part, src_lang, dest_lang, provider=provider,
140	                              domain=domain, source=source)
141	        if arr is None:
142	            ColorPrint.yellow(
143	                f"[ai_batch_translate] {src_lang}->{dest_lang} failed on a chunk of {len(part)}")
144	            return None
145	        out.extend(arr)
146	        if progress:
147	            try:
148	                progress(min(start + len(part), total), total)
149	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - progress is best-effort
150	                pass
151	    return out
152

All three mapping reports are accurate against the live source (verified file:line). A role=client codesync node today runs 4 long-lived background threads plus the HTTP/WS server, even though a client is fully PASSIVE: (1) Stats refresher `CodeSync-Stats` (manager.py:62/165) — full-tree os.scandir walk every 60s (STATS_REFRESH_SECONDS, manager.py:43), the single biggest recurring CPU/IO cost and used ONLY to fill the UI `code` block; (2) PeerMesh loop `CodeSync-PeerMesh` (manager.py:68; peer_mesh.py:74/100) — wakes every 5s (TICK_SECONDS, peer_mesh.py:39) but for a client `tick()` skips all outbound probe/heartbeat (peer_mesh.py:119, 156), so it only snapshots+emits; (3) PushSender supervisor `CodeSync-WsPush` (manager.py:81; sync_ws.py:428/439) — started unconditionally but `is_distributing()` is always False on a client (manager.py:127), so it idle-rechecks doing nothing; (4) the ThreadingHTTPServer `CodeSync-HTTP` (http_server.py:1030) that serves the WS receiver AND the ~585-line inline HTML control PANEL at GET / (http_server.py:822/825) — standalone only. The PushReceiver (manager.py:79; sync_ws.py:123) has NO own thread (runs on the per-connection WS handler, http_server.py:794) and is the client's actual job. The legacy CodeSyncClient LAN puller (client.py) is already dead on the client path (manager.py:104-119). Full-pycore reuses the SAME manager + 3 threads; it never starts the standalone server/panel, instead serving /code-sync/ws via _register_code_sync_ws (config.py:699). No --light/CODESYNC_LIGHT knob exists today. Here's what I'm locking in:

| Decision | Locked choice |
|---|---|
| Scope | **Client modifier** (no-op + warning on dev) |
| Role | **Opt-in**, never forces `role=client` |
| Mesh probe | **30s** (90s stale window) vs 5s |
| Client stats | **Static zeros** — drop the 60s scan thread entirely |
| `GET /` panel | **Tiny status JSON** (inspectable, no 585-line HTML) |
| `/file-tree` | **`{success:false,light:true}`**, never spawns the watcher |
| Toggle | **Restart-only** boot flag + read-only UI badge |
| full-pycore | **Node-local** `CODESYNC_LIGHT` env (not replicated) |

## 2. codesync runtime light mode ✅
A discovery pass found a `role=client` node runs **4 long-lived threads while fully passive**. Built an opt-in `--light` / `CODESYNC_LIGHT=1` / `configure(light=True)` mode (single source of truth `runtime.is_light()`, set before the first `get_manager()`):

- **Trims** (client only): drop the 60s full-tree stats scan (static zeros), never start the push-sender supervisor, panel off → tiny status JSON at `GET /`, `/file-tree` light no-op (can't resurrect the watcher), mesh probe 5s→30s, sync-log ring 300→50.
- **Keeps intact**: WS receiver + `/code-sync/ws`, HTTP/FastAPI server + JSON status routes, PeerMesh presence, LWW config replication, receive path.
- **Hard invariant** (the one bug review caught and I fixed): every trim — *including* the daemon-level panel trim — is gated on `light AND role=='client'`; a light-flagged **dev** takes none of the trims (warn + no-op), so it can still distribute.
- Default OFF → existing nodes byte-for-byte unchanged. Surfaced as `light:true` in status/CLI and a read-only "Light" badge in `PcCodeSyncPage.tsx`.

8 Python files + 2 UI files; `py_compile` clean across all codesync files, `tsc` zero new errors. Recorded both the AI-provider detail and the new codesync mode to memory.

# How Claude remembers your project

> Give Claude persistent instructions with CLAUDE.md files, and let Claude accumulate learnings automatically with auto memory.

Each Claude Code session begins with a fresh context window. Two mechanisms carry knowledge across sessions:

* **CLAUDE.md files**: instructions you write to give Claude persistent context
* **Auto memory**: notes Claude writes itself based on your corrections and preferences

This page covers how to:

* [Write and organize CLAUDE.md files](#claude-md-files)
* [Scope rules to specific file types](#organize-rules-with-claude/rules/) with `.claude/rules/`
* [Configure auto memory](#auto-memory) so Claude takes notes automatically
* [Troubleshoot](#troubleshoot-memory-issues) when instructions aren't being followed

## CLAUDE.md vs auto memory

Claude Code has two complementary memory systems. Both are loaded at the start of every conversation. Claude treats them as context, not enforced configuration. To block an action regardless of what Claude decides, use a [PreToolUse hook](/en/hooks-guide) instead. The more specific and concise your instructions, the more consistently Claude follows them.

|                      | CLAUDE.md files                                   | Auto memory                                                      |
| :------------------- | :------------------------------------------------ | :--------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Who writes it**    | You                                               | Claude                                                           |
| **What it contains** | Instructions and rules                            | Learnings and patterns                                           |
| **Scope**            | Project, user, or org

# CURSOR — `.cursor/rules/*.mdc`

**Source:** https://cursor.com/docs/context/rules (live page; content rendered client-side, extracted from the page payload)

## Activation model (rule types)
Cursor's docs state: **"Cursor supports four types of rules:"** Project Rules, User Rules, Team Rules (Enterprise), and AGENTS.md. For project rules (`.mdc`), the docs give a "Rule Type" table mapping the four activation modes (note: Cursor has **renamed** the old labels — the new official names are in the left column, old names in parentheses below):

| Rule Type (official) | Activation (exact wording) |
|---|---|
| **Always Apply** (was "Always") | "Apply to every chat session" |
| **Apply Intelligently** (was "Agent Requested") | "When Agent decides it's relevant based on description" |
| **Apply to Specific Files** (was "Auto Attached") | "When file matches a specified pattern" |
| **Apply Manually** (was "Manual") | invoked with `@my-rule` |

The terms "Auto Attached" / "Agent Requested" you asked about are the **older** names; the current docs use Always Apply / Apply Intelligently / Apply to Specific Files / Apply Manually. The underlying frontmatter fields are unchanged.

## Frontmatter fields and how they interact
Docs: *"Control how rules are applied from the type dropdown which changes properties `description`, `globs`, `alwaysApply`."* The behavior table (exact wording):

| `alwaysApply` | `description` | `globs` | Behavior |
|---|---|---|---|
| `true` | — | — | **"Always included. Globs and description are ignored."** |
| `false` | — | provided | **"Auto-attached when a matching file is in context."** |
| `false` | provided | omitted | **"Agent reads the description and pulls the rule in when relevant."** |
| `false` | omitted | omitted | (Manual) invoked via `@` |
